        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.

````md
## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small

   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

1. **Generalize prompt builders** → parameterize by form ID (currently hardcoded to "scene"); inject form-specific lenses

```
194	def get_check_ids() -> list[str]:
195	    return [check["id"] for check in CRITIQUE_CHECKS]
196	
197	
198	def get_check_by_id(check_id: str) -> CritiqueCheckSpec | None:
199	    return _CHECK_BY_ID.get(check_id)
200	
201	
202	def build_check_category_map() -> dict[str, str]:
203	    return {check["id"]: check["category"] for check in CRITIQUE_CHECKS}
204	
205	
206	def checks_for_robustness(robustness: str) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]:
207	    if robustness in {"robust", "superrobust"}:
208	        return CRITIQUE_CHECKS
209	    if robustness in {"light", "tiny"}:
210	        return ()
211	    return _CORE_CRITIQUE_CHECKS
212	
213	
214	def joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness: str) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]:
215	    if robustness == "tiny":

217	    return JOKE_CRITIQUE_CHECKS
218	
219	
220	def build_empty_template(checks: tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...] | None = None) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
221	    active_checks = CRITIQUE_CHECKS if checks is None else checks
222	    return [
223	        {
224	            "id": check["id"],
225	            "question": check["question"],
226	            "findings": [],
227	        }
228	        for check in active_checks
229	    ]
230	
231	
232	_MIN_FINDING_DETAIL_LENGTH = 40  # Must describe what was checked, not just "No issue"
233	
234	
235	def _valid_findings(findings: Any) -> bool:
236	    if not isinstance(findings, list) or not findings:
237	        return False
238	    for finding in findings:
239	        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
240	            return False
241	        detail = finding.get("detail")
242	        flagged = finding.get("flagged")
243	        if not isinstance(detail, str) or not detail.strip():
244	            return False
245	        if len(detail.strip()) < _MIN_FINDING_DETAIL_LENGTH:
246	            return False
247	        if not isinstance(flagged, bool):
248	            return False
249	    return True
250	
251	
252	def validate_critique_checks(
253	    payload: Any,
254	    *,
255	    expected_ids: tuple[str, ...] | list[str] | None = None,
256	) -> list[str]:
257	    raw_checks = payload.get("checks") if isinstance(payload, dict) else payload
258	    expected = get_check_ids() if expected_ids is None else list(expected_ids)
259	    if not isinstance(raw_checks, list):
260	        return expected
261	
262	    expected_set = set(expected)
263	    valid_ids: set[str] = set()
264	    invalid_expected_ids: set[str] = set()
265	    invalid_unknown_ids: set[str] = set()
266	    seen_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
267	
268	    for raw_check in raw_checks:
269	        if not isinstance(raw_check, dict):
270	            continue
271	        check_id = raw_check.get("id")
272	        if not isinstance(check_id, str) or not check_id:
273	            continue
274	
275	        seen_counts[check_id] = seen_counts.get(check_id, 0) + 1
276	        if check_id not in expected_set:
277	            invalid_unknown_ids.add(check_id)
278	            continue
279	        if seen_counts[check_id] > 1:
280	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
281	            continue
282	
283	        question = raw_check.get("question")
284	        findings = raw_check.get("findings")
285	        if not isinstance(question, str) or not question.strip():
286	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
287	            continue
288	        if not _valid_findings(findings):
289	            invalid_expected_ids.add(check_id)
290	            continue
291	        valid_ids.add(check_id)
292	
293	    return [
294	        check_id
295	        for check_id in expected
296	        if check_id not in valid_ids or check_id in invalid_expected_ids
297	    ] + sorted(invalid_unknown_ids)
298	
299	
300	__all__ = [
301	    "CRITIQUE_CHECKS",
302	    "JOKE_CRITIQUE_CHECKS",
```

```
8	from megaplan.doc_assembly import extract_settled_decisions
9	from megaplan.profiles import apply_profile_expansion
10	from megaplan.types import ROBUSTNESS_LEVELS, CliError, PlanState, STATE_INITIALIZED, StepResponse
11	from megaplan._core import (
12	    append_history,

24	from .shared import _append_to_meta, _attach_next_step_runtime, _validate_relative_path
25	
26	def handle_init(root: Path, args: argparse.Namespace) -> StepResponse:
27	    ensure_runtime_layout(root)
28	    project_dir = Path(args.project_dir).expanduser().resolve()
29	    if not project_dir.exists() or not project_dir.is_dir():
30	        raise CliError("invalid_project_dir", f"Project directory does not exist: {project_dir}")
31	    apply_profile_expansion(args, project_dir)
32	    positional_idea = getattr(args, "idea", None)
33	    idea_file = getattr(args, "idea_file", None)
34	    if positional_idea and idea_file:
35	        raise CliError("invalid_args", "Pass either the positional idea or --idea-file, not both")
36	    if idea_file:
37	        idea_path = Path(idea_file).expanduser().resolve()
38	        try:
39	            idea_text = idea_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
40	        except OSError as exc:
41	            raise CliError("invalid_args", f"Unable to read --idea-file {idea_path}: {exc}") from exc
42	        if not idea_text:
43	            raise CliError("invalid_args", "--idea-file must contain non-empty UTF-8 text")
44	    elif positional_idea:
45	        idea_text = positional_idea
46	    else:
47	        raise CliError("invalid_args", "Provide an idea argument or --idea-file <path>")
48	    explicit_mode = getattr(args, "mode", None)
49	    raw_output_path = getattr(args, "output", None)
50	    raw_primary_criterion = getattr(args, "primary_criterion", None)
51	    mode = explicit_mode or "code"
52	    if mode == "metaplan":
53	        mode = "doc"
54	    if raw_primary_criterion and mode != "joke":
55	        raise CliError("invalid_args", "--primary-criterion is only valid with --mode joke")
56	
57	    if mode == "code" and raw_output_path:
58	        raise CliError(
59	            "invalid_args",
60	            "--output is only valid with --mode doc or --mode joke. For code-mode runs, remove "
61	            "--output; for prose artifact runs, also pass --mode doc or --mode joke.",
62	        )
63	    normalized_output_path: str | None = None
64	    if mode in {"doc", "joke"} and not raw_output_path:
65	        raise CliError("invalid_args", f"--output is required when --mode {mode} is selected")
66	    if raw_output_path:
67	        normalized_output_path = _validate_relative_path(project_dir, raw_output_path, "--output")
68	    normalized_primary_criterion: str | None = None
69	    if raw_primary_criterion is not None:
70	        normalized_primary_criterion = str(raw_primary_criterion).strip()
71	        if not normalized_primary_criterion:
72	            raise CliError("invalid_args", "--primary-criterion must be non-empty when provided")
73	    raw_from_doc = getattr(args, "from_doc", None)
74	    from_doc_rel: str | None = None
75	    imported_decisions: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
76	    parse_warnings: list[str] = []
77	    if raw_from_doc:
78	        from_doc_rel = _validate_relative_path(project_dir, raw_from_doc, "--from-doc")
79	        from_doc_abs = project_dir / from_doc_rel
80	        if not from_doc_abs.exists() or not from_doc_abs.is_file():
81	            raise CliError("invalid_args", f"--from-doc path does not exist: {from_doc_rel}")
82	        imported_decisions, parse_warnings = extract_settled_decisions(
83	            from_doc_abs.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
84	        )
85	    robustness = getattr(args, "robustness", None)
86	    if robustness is None:
87	        robustness = get_effective("execution", "robustness")
88	    if robustness not in ROBUSTNESS_LEVELS:
89	        robustness = "standard"
90	    auto_approve_value = getattr(args, "auto_approve", None)
91	    if auto_approve_value is None:
92	        auto_approve_value = get_effective("execution", "auto_approve")
93	    auto_approve = bool(auto_approve_value)
94	    timestamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M")
95	    plan_name = args.name or f"{slugify(idea_text)}-{timestamp}"
96	    plan_dir = plans_root(root) / plan_name
97	    if plan_dir.exists():
98	        raise CliError("duplicate_plan", f"Plan directory already exists: {plan_name}")
99	    plan_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
100

103	        "idea": idea_text,
104	        "current_state": STATE_INITIALIZED,

134	    if from_doc_rel is not None:
```

57	    """
58	).strip()
59	
60	
61	def _primary_criterion(state: PlanState) -> str:
62	    criterion = state.get("config", {}).get("primary_criterion", "")
63	    if isinstance(criterion, str) and criterion.strip():
64	        return criterion.strip()

68	def _execute_joke_requirements(checkpoint_path: str) -> str:
69	    return textwrap.dedent(
70	        f"""
71	        Requirements:
72	        - You are a screenwriter authoring a single scene, not a coder shipping source edits.
73	        - Write the final scene prose to the configured output path. This is the only file you should create or modify.
74	        - The primary criterion is load-bearing. Keep the scene weird/coherent/bathetic according to that declared target.
75	        - `sections_written` should use scene-beat IDs such as `opening`, `inciting`, `obstacle`, `turn`, and `button`.
76	        - Use the tasks in `finalize.json` as the execution boundary.
77	        - Best-effort progress checkpointing: if `{checkpoint_path}` is writable, then after each completed task read the full file, update that task's `status`, `executor_notes`, and `sections_written`, and write the full file back. Do NOT write to `finalize.json` directly.
78	        - Best-effort sense-check checkpointing: if `{checkpoint_path}` is writable, then after each sense check acknowledgment read the full file again, update that sense check's `executor_note`, and write the full file back.
79	        - Return `task_updates` with one object per completed or skipped task.
80	        - `task_updates[].status` must be either `done` or `skipped`. Never return `pending` in execute output.
81	        - Return `sense_check_acknowledgments` with one object per sense check.
82	        - Keep `executor_notes` verification-focused: explain why the scene beats and final prose satisfy the brief and primary criterion.
83	        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
84	        {_EXECUTE_JOKE_OUTPUT_SHAPE_EXAMPLE}
85	        """
86	    ).strip()
87	
88	
89	def _execute_joke_prompt(state: PlanState, plan_dir: Path, root: Path | None = None) -> str:
90	    project_dir = Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])
91	    output_path = state["config"].get("output_path", "output.md")
92	    primary_criterion = _primary_criterion(state)
93	    prep_block, prep_instruction = _render_prep_block(plan_dir)
94	    finalize_data = read_json(plan_dir / "finalize.json")
95	    checkpoint_path = str(plan_dir / "execution_checkpoint.json")
96	    latest_meta = read_json(latest_plan_meta_path(plan_dir, state))
97	    gate = read_json(plan_dir / "gate.json")
98	    robustness = configured_robustness(state)
99	    prior_review_block = _execute_review_block(plan_dir)
100	    rerun_guidance = _execute_rerun_guidance(plan_dir, finalize_data)
101	    approval_note = _execute_approval_note(state)
102	    execution_nudges = _execute_nudges(finalize_data, plan_dir, root)
103	    requirements_block = _execute_joke_requirements(checkpoint_path)
104	    return textwrap.dedent(
105	        f"""
106	        Author the planned joke-mode scene prose.
107	
108	        Project directory:
109	        {project_dir}
110	
111	        Output path (write the scene here):
112	        {output_path}
113	
114	        Primary criterion:
115	        {primary_criterion}
116	
117	        {prep_block}
118	
119	        {prep_instruction}
120	
121	        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}
122	
123	        Execution tracking source of truth (`finalize.json`):
124	        {json_dump(finalize_data).strip()}
125	
126	        Absolute checkpoint path for best-effort progress checkpoints (NOT `finalize.json`):
127	        {checkpoint_path}
128	
129	        Plan metadata:
130	        {json_dump(latest_meta).strip()}
131	
132	        Gate summary:
133	        {json_dump(gate).strip()}
134	
135	        {prior_review_block}
136	
137	        {rerun_guidance}
138	
139	        {approval_note}
140	        Robustness level: {robustness}.
141	
142	        {requirements_block}
143	
144	        {execution_nudges}
145	        """
146	    ).strip()
147	
148	
149	def _execute_joke_batch_prompt(
150	    state: PlanState,
151	    plan_dir: Path,
152	    batch_task_ids: list[str],
153	    completed_task_ids: set[str] | None = None,
154	    root: Path | None = None,
155	) -> str:
156	    completed = set(completed_task_ids or set())
157	    output_path = state["config"].get("output_path", "output.md")
158	    primary_criterion = _primary_criterion(state)
159	    finalize_data = read_json(plan_dir / "finalize.json")
160	    all_tasks = finalize_data.get("tasks", [])
161	    tasks_by_id = {
162	        task["id"]: task
163	        for task in all_tasks
164	        if isinstance(task, dict) and isinstance(task.get("id"), str)
165	    }
166	    batch_tasks = [tasks_by_id[task_id] for task_id in batch_task_ids if task_id in tasks_by_id]
167	    completed_tasks = [
168	        task for task_id, task in tasks_by_id.items() if task_id in completed and task_id not in set(batch_task_ids)
169	    ]
170	    batch_sense_checks = [
171	        sense_check
172	        for sense_check in finalize_data.get("sense_checks", [])
173	        if sense_check.get("task_id") in set(batch_task_ids)
174	    ]
175	    batch_sense_check_ids = [
176	        sense_check["id"] for sense_check in batch_sense_checks if isinstance(sense_check.get("id"), str)
177	    ]
178	    global_batches = compute_task_batches(all_tasks)
179	    batch_number = next((index + 1 for index, batch in enumerate(global_batches) if batch == batch_task_ids), 1)
180	    batch_total = len(global_batches) or 1
181	    checkpoint_path = str(batch_artifact_path(plan_dir, batch_number))
182	    prior_batch_deviations = "None"
183	    if batch_number > 1:
184	        prior_batch_artifact = batch_artifact_path(plan_dir, batch_number - 1)
185	        if prior_batch_artifact.exists():
186	            try:
187	                prior_batch_payload = read_json(prior_batch_artifact)
188	            except (OSError, ValueError):
189	                prior_batch_payload = {}
190	            raw_deviations = prior_batch_payload.get("deviations", [])
191	            if isinstance(raw_deviations, list):
192	                deviations = [item for item in raw_deviations if isinstance(item, str)]
193	                if deviations:
194	                    prior_batch_deviations = json_dump(deviations).strip()
195	    approval_note = _execute_approval_note(state)
196	    debt_watch_items = _debt_watch_lines(plan_dir, root)
197	    debt_watch_block = (
198	        "\n".join(["Debt watch items (do not make these worse):", *[f"- {item}" for item in debt_watch_items]])
199	        if debt_watch_items
200	        else "Debt watch items (do not make these worse):\n- None."
201	    )
202	    return textwrap.dedent(
203	        f"""
204	        Author the planned joke-mode scene prose.
205	
206	        Project directory:
207	        {Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])}
208	
209	        Output path (write the scene here):
210	        {output_path}
211	
212	        Primary criterion:
213	        {primary_criterion}
214	
215	        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}
216	
217	        Batch framing:
218	        - Execute batch {batch_number} of {batch_total}.
219	        - Actionable task IDs for this batch: {batch_task_ids}
220	        - Already completed task IDs available as dependency context: {sorted(completed)}
221	
222	        Actionable tasks for this batch:
223	        {json_dump(batch_tasks).strip()}
224	
225	        Completed task context (already satisfied, do not re-execute unless directly required by current edits):
226	        {json_dump(completed_tasks).strip()}
227	
228	        Prior batch deviations (address if applicable):
229	        {prior_batch_deviations}
230	
231	        Batch-scoped sense checks:
232	        {json_dump(batch_sense_checks).strip()}
233	
234	        Full execution tracking source of truth (`finalize.json`):
235	        {json_dump(finalize_data).strip()}
236	
237	        {debt_watch_block}
238	
239	        {approval_note}
240	        Robustness level: {configured_robustness(state)}.
241	
242	        Requirements:
243	        - You are a screenwriter authoring a single scene.
244	        - Execute only the actionable tasks in this batch.
245	        - Treat completed tasks as dependency context, not new work.
246	        - Return structured JSON only.
247	        - Only produce `task_updates` for these tasks: [{", ".join(batch_task_ids)}]
248	        - Only produce `sense_check_acknowledgments` for these sense checks: [{", ".join(batch_sense_check_ids)}]
249	        - Do not include updates for tasks or sense checks outside this batch.
250	        - Keep `executor_notes` verification-focused.
251	        - Best-effort progress checkpointing: if `{checkpoint_path}` is writable, checkpoint task and sense-check updates there (not `finalize.json`). The harness owns `finalize.json`.
252	        - `sections_written` means scene-beat IDs such as `opening`, `inciting`, `obstacle`, `turn`, and `button`.
253	        - The final artifact written to `{output_path}` must be scene prose, not an outline or task list.
254	        - Follow this JSON shape:
255	        {_EXECUTE_JOKE_OUTPUT_SHAPE_EXAMPLE}
256	        """
257	    ).strip()
258	
259	
260	__all__ = ["_execute_joke_prompt", "_execute_joke_batch_prompt"]
261

```
18	def _prep_joke_prompt(state: PlanState, plan_dir: Path, root: Path | None = None) -> str:
19	    del root
20	    project_dir = Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])
21	    output_path = plan_dir / "prep.json"
22	    declared_primary_criterion = _declared_primary_criterion(state)
23	    declared_primary_criterion_block = ""
24	    if declared_primary_criterion:
25	        declared_primary_criterion_block = textwrap.dedent(
26	            f"""
27	            Declared primary criterion:
28	            {declared_primary_criterion}
29	
30	            Preserve this unless the task text clearly contradicts it.
31	            """
32	        ).strip()
33	    return textwrap.dedent(
34	        f"""
35	        Prepare a concise scene-writing brief for the joke-mode task below. This brief will be the primary context for all subsequent planning and execution.
36	
37	        Task:
38	        {state["idea"]}
39	
40	        Project: {project_dir}
41	        Output file: {output_path}
42	
43	        {declared_primary_criterion_block}
44	
45	        First, assess: does this task need investigation?
46	
47	        Set "skip": true if ALL of these are true:
48	        - The scene premise, tone, and comic target are already explicit.
49	        - The primary criterion is already obvious from the task.
50	        - No repository context, prior examples, or tonal references need review.
51	
52	        Set "skip": false if ANY of these are true:
53	        - The brief needs film, genre, or tonal anchor references.
54	        - The scene premise is underspecified or could go in multiple directions.
55	        - The primary criterion is not explicit and must be inferred from context.
56	        - Characters, location, constraints, or format expectations are still fuzzy.
57	
58	        If skipping, leave everything else empty. The original task description will be used directly.
59	        If not skipping, fill in the brief:
60	        1. Summarize the scene premise and the comic engine.
61	        2. Declare the primary criterion for the scene. This field is REQUIRED when skip is false.
62	        3. Identify useful reference films, tonal anchors, or comparable scenes.
63	        4. Name the likely characters, location, and core dramatic situation.
64	        5. Capture constraints such as length, rating, tone, and format.
65	
66	        Brief fields:
67	        - skip: true if no investigation is needed, false if the brief has useful content.
68	        - task_summary: What the scene should accomplish, in 2-3 sentences.
69	        - primary_criterion: REQUIRED when skip is false. Examples: "weirdest coherent", "most bathetic", "darkest that plays funny".
70	        - key_evidence: Facts from the task description or repository context that shape the scene.
71	        - relevant_code: File paths or existing artifacts worth referencing for continuity or house style. Use this for source material, not code to change.
72	        - test_expectations: For joke mode, list the scene beats or constraints reviewers should verify. Each entry's test_id is a beat or requirement name, status is "expected", and what_it_checks describes what the scene must deliver.
73	        - constraints: Length, rating, tone, location, format, or content constraints that must be preserved.
74	        - suggested_approach: A concrete scene-writing approach grounded in what you found.
75	
76	        Return JSON only.
77	        """
78	    ).strip()
79	
80	
81	__all__ = ["_prep_joke_prompt"]
```

```
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import textwrap
6	from pathlib import Path
7	from typing import Any
8	
9	from megaplan.audits.robustness import joke_checks_for_robustness
10	from megaplan.types import PlanState
11	
12	from .critique import _build_critique_prompt, _critique_context, _write_critique_template
13	
14	
15	def _primary_criterion(state: PlanState) -> str:
16	    criterion = state.get("config", {}).get("primary_criterion", "")
17	    if isinstance(criterion, str) and criterion.strip():
18	        return criterion.strip()
19	    return "[missing primary criterion]"
20	
21	
22	def _lens_quality(check: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
23	    return str(check.get("id", "lens")).replace("_", " ")
24	
25	
26	def _joke_lens_instruction(check: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
27	    lens = str(check["id"])
28	    quality = _lens_quality(check)
29	    return textwrap.dedent(
30	        f"""
31	        Persona: {lens}
32	        Question: {check["question"]}
33	        Guidance: {check.get("guidance", "")}
34	        Your persona is `{lens}`. Propose exactly one concrete, named, committed beat change that would make this scene maximally {quality} while still serving the primary criterion.
35	        Do not hedge.
36	        Do not suggest multiple alternatives.
37	        Write the proposal as if you are committing to it: specific character, line, prop, turn, or button.
38	        Avoid meta-commentary.
39	        Output one `FLAG-{lens}` in the `flags` array for this lens and do not leave `findings` empty.
40	        """
41	    ).strip()
42	
43	
44	def _joke_findings_requirements() -> str:
45	    return textwrap.dedent(
46	        """
47	        For each active lens:
48	        - Add at least one finding to that check's `findings` array.
49	        - Keep the existing findings schema unchanged: each finding needs `detail` and `flagged`.
50	        - Use `detail` to describe the exact committed proposal and why it serves the primary criterion.
51	        - Set `flagged` to true for the committed proposal.
52	
53	        For the `flags` array:
54	        - Emit one committed generative FLAG per active lens.
55	        - Use the exact ID format `FLAG-<lens>`.
56	        - Make the concern concrete and named: beat, line, prop, reveal, turn, or button.
57	        - Do not hedge. Do not say "consider". Do not offer multiple options.
58	        """
59	    ).strip()
60	
61	
62	def _critique_joke_prompt(state: PlanState, plan_dir: Path, root: Path | None = None) -> str:
63	    context = _critique_context(state, plan_dir, root)
64	    active_checks = joke_checks_for_robustness(context["robustness"])
65	    output_path = _write_critique_template(plan_dir, state, active_checks)
66	    primary_criterion = _primary_criterion(state)
67	    iteration = state.get("iteration", 1)
68	
69	    if active_checks:
70	        iteration_context = ""
71	        if iteration > 1:
72	            iteration_context = (
73	                "\n\n            This is critique iteration {iteration}. "
74	                "The template file includes prior findings with their status. "
75	                "Keep each lens distinct, verify whether prior proposals still earn their place, "
76	                "and avoid collapsing multiple personas into the same move."
77	            ).format(iteration=iteration)
78	        lens_block = "\n\n".join(_joke_lens_instruction(check) for check in active_checks)
79	        critique_review_block = textwrap.dedent(
80	            f"""
81	            Your output template is at: {output_path}
82	            Read this file first — it contains {len(active_checks)} joke-lens checks, each with a question and guidance.
83	            This is the sequential fallback path: run every lens persona in one pass without inventing new infrastructure.
84	
85	            The primary criterion for this scene is: {primary_criterion}
86	
87	            {_joke_findings_requirements()}
88	
89	            Active lens personas:
90	            {lens_block}
91	
92	            Workflow: read the file → investigate the plan and repository context → read the file again → write one committed proposal per lens into both `findings` and `flags` → write the file back.{iteration_context}
93	        """
94	        ).strip()
95	    else:
96	        critique_review_block = textwrap.dedent(
97	            f"""
98	            Your output template is at: {output_path}
99	            No joke-lens checks are active at this robustness level.
100	            The primary criterion for this scene is: {primary_criterion}
101	
102	            Review the plan broadly through a generative lens. If you identify a concrete beat, line, prop, turn, button, or reveal that would better serve the primary criterion, record it in the `flags` array using a committed proposal rather than a hedge.
103	
104	            Workflow: read the file → investigate → read the file again → add any concrete generative flags → write the file back.
105	        """
106	        ).strip()
107	    return _build_critique_prompt(state, context, critique_review_block)
108	
109	
110	def single_check_critique_joke_prompt(
111	    state: PlanState,
112	    plan_dir: Path,
113	    root: Path | None,
114	    check: dict[str, Any],

116	) -> str:

132	
133	        The primary criterion for this scene is: {primary_criterion}
134	
135	        {_joke_lens_instruction(check)}

137	        Findings requirements:
138	        - Keep the existing findings schema unchanged: each finding needs `detail` and `flagged`.
139	        - Add at least one finding to this check's `findings` array.
140	        - Use `detail` to describe the exact committed proposal and why it serves the primary criterion.
141	        - Set `flagged` to true for the committed proposal.
142	
143	        Flag requirements:
144	        - Emit exactly one `FLAG-{check["id"]}` entry in the `flags` array for this lens.
145	        - Make the proposal concrete and named: specific character, line, prop, reveal, turn, or button.
146	        - Do not hedge.
147	        - Do not suggest multiple alternatives.
148	        - Avoid meta-commentary.
149	
150	        Workflow: read the file → investigate → read the file again → add one committed proposal to `findings` and one committed `FLAG-{check["id"]}` to `flags` → write the file back.{iteration_context}
151	    """
152	    ).strip()
153	    return _build_critique_prompt(state, context, critique_review_block)
```

1	"""Joke-mode review prompt builder."""

3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import textwrap
6	from pathlib import Path
7	from typing import Any
8	
9	from megaplan._core import (
10	    intent_and_notes_block,
11	    json_dump,

17	from megaplan.types import PlanState
18	
19	from .review import _settled_decisions_block, _settled_decisions_instruction
20	
21	
22	def _primary_criterion(state: PlanState) -> str:
23	    criterion = state.get("config", {}).get("primary_criterion", "")
24	    if isinstance(criterion, str) and criterion.strip():
25	        return criterion.strip()
26	    return "[missing primary criterion]"
27	
28	
29	def _review_joke_prompt(
30	    state: PlanState,
31	    plan_dir: Path,
32	    *,
33	    review_intro: str,
34	    criteria_guidance: str,
35	    task_guidance: str,
36	    sense_check_guidance: str,
37	    pre_check_flags: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
38	) -> str:
39	    project_dir = Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])
40	    output_path = state["config"].get("output_path", "output.md")
41	    primary_criterion = _primary_criterion(state)
42	    resolved_output = Path(project_dir) / output_path
43	    if resolved_output.exists():
44	        try:
45	            output_content = resolved_output.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
46	        except OSError:
47	            output_content = "(could not read output file)"
48	    else:
49	        output_content = "(output file does not exist yet)"
50	
51	    latest_plan = latest_plan_path(plan_dir, state).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
52	    latest_meta = read_json(latest_plan_meta_path(plan_dir, state))
53	    execution = read_json(plan_dir / "execution.json")
54	    gate = read_json(plan_dir / "gate.json")
55	    finalize_data = read_json(plan_dir / "finalize.json")
56	    settled_decisions_block = _settled_decisions_block(gate)
57	    settled_decisions_instruction = _settled_decisions_instruction(gate)
58	
59	    flag_reverify_items: list[dict[str, str]] = []
60	    for flag in load_flag_registry(plan_dir).get("flags", []):
61	        if not isinstance(flag, dict):
62	            continue
63	        status = str(flag.get("status", "open"))
64	        if status not in {"open", "addressed", "verified", "disputed"}:
65	            continue
66	        flag_reverify_items.append(
67	            {
68	                "id": str(flag.get("id", "")),
69	                "concern": str(flag.get("concern", "")),
70	                "severity": str(flag.get("severity") or flag.get("severity_hint") or "uncertain"),
71	                "status": status,
72	            }
73	        )
74	    flag_reverify_block = ""
75	    if flag_reverify_items:
76	        flag_reverify_block = textwrap.dedent(
77	            f"""
78	            Critique flags to re-verify against the output scene:
79	            {json_dump(flag_reverify_items).strip()}
80	
81	            For each flag above, verify whether the final scene actually resolves the concern while preserving the primary criterion.
82	            Add resolved flag IDs to `verified_flag_ids`.
83	            For any unresolved flag, add a `rework_items` entry with `task_id: "REVIEW"`, `issue`, `expected`, `actual`, `evidence_file`, `flag_id`, and `source: "review_flag_reverify"`.
84	            """
85	        ).strip()
86	    pre_check_block = ""
87	    if pre_check_flags:
88	        pre_check_block = textwrap.dedent(
89	            f"""
90	            Advisory mechanical pre-check flags:
91	            {json_dump(pre_check_flags).strip()}
92	
93	            Copy this list verbatim into the output `pre_check_flags` field.
94	            """
95	        ).strip()
96	    extra_sections = ""
97	    if flag_reverify_block:
98	        extra_sections += f"\n\n{flag_reverify_block}"
99	    if pre_check_block:
100	        extra_sections += f"\n\n{pre_check_block}"
101	
102	    return textwrap.dedent(
103	        f"""
104	        {review_intro}
105	
106	        Project directory:
107	        {project_dir}
108	
109	        Output file path:
110	        {output_path}
111	
112	        Primary criterion:
113	        {primary_criterion}
114	
115	        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}
116	
117	        Approved scene canvas:
118	        {latest_plan}
119	
120	        Execution tracking state (`finalize.json`):
121	        {json_dump(finalize_data).strip()}
122	
123	        Plan metadata:
124	        {json_dump(latest_meta).strip()}
125	
126	        Gate summary:
127	        {json_dump(gate).strip()}
128	
129	        {settled_decisions_block}{extra_sections}
130	
131	        Execution summary:
132	        {json_dump(execution).strip()}
133	
134	        Output scene content:
135	        {output_content}
136	
137	        Requirements:
138	        - Read the output file above and judge it first against the declared primary criterion, then against the remaining success criteria and the approved scene canvas.
139	        - {criteria_guidance}
140	        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the scene text or vague notes make a claim ambiguous.
141	        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
142	          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
143	          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
144	          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
145	          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context, mark it `waived` with an explanation.
146	        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or the scene prose is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
147	        {settled_decisions_instruction}
148	        - {task_guidance}
149	        - {sense_check_guidance}
150	        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
151	        ```json
152	        {{
153	          "review_verdict": "approved",
154	          "criteria": [
155	            {{
156	              "name": "Scene serves the declared primary criterion",
157	              "priority": "must",
158	              "pass": "pass",
159	              "evidence": "The final scene preserves the intended comic engine from opening through button."
160	            }}
161	          ],
162	          "issues": [],
163	          "rework_items": [],
164	          "summary": "Approved. The scene meets the brief and the primary criterion.",
165	          "task_verdicts": [
166	            {{
167	              "task_id": "T1",
168	              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. The written scene beats align with the planned canvas and primary criterion.",
169	              "evidence_files": []
170	            }}
171	          ],
172	          "sense_check_verdicts": [
173	            {{
174	              "sense_check_id": "SC1",
175	              "verdict": "Confirmed. The final scene still serves the declared primary criterion."
176	            }}
177	          ]
178	        }}
179	        ```
180	        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue.
181	        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items`.
182	        - When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
183	        """
184	    ).strip()

```
24	def _primary_criterion(state: PlanState) -> str:
25	    criterion = state.get("config", {}).get("primary_criterion", "")
26	    if isinstance(criterion, str) and criterion.strip():
27	        return criterion.strip()
28	    return "[missing primary criterion]"

30	
31	def _revise_joke_prompt(state: PlanState, plan_dir: Path) -> str:
32	    project_dir = Path(state["config"]["project_dir"])
33	    prep_block, prep_instruction = _render_prep_block(plan_dir)
34	    latest_plan = latest_plan_path(plan_dir, state).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
35	    latest_meta = read_json(latest_plan_meta_path(plan_dir, state))
36	    gate = read_json(plan_dir / "gate.json")
37	    unresolved = unresolved_significant_flags(load_flag_registry(plan_dir))
38	    open_flags = [
39	        {
40	            "id": flag["id"],
41	            "severity": flag.get("severity"),
42	            "status": flag["status"],
43	            "concern": flag["concern"],
44	            "evidence": flag.get("evidence"),
45	        }
46	        for flag in unresolved
47	    ]
48	    settled_decisions: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
49	    decisions_path = plan_dir / "tiebreaker_decisions.json"
50	    if decisions_path.exists():
51	        decisions_data = read_json(decisions_path)
52	        if isinstance(decisions_data, list):
53	            settled_decisions = decisions_data
54	        elif isinstance(decisions_data, dict):
55	            settled_decisions = decisions_data.get("decisions", [])
56	    settled_block = _settled_decisions_block(settled_decisions)
57	    primary_criterion = _primary_criterion(state)
58	
59	    return textwrap.dedent(
60	        f"""
61	        You are revising a joke-mode scene plan after critique and gate feedback.
62	
63	        Project directory:
64	        {project_dir}
65	
66	        {prep_block}
67	        {prep_instruction}
68	
69	        {intent_and_notes_block(state)}
70	
71	        Current plan (markdown):
72	        {latest_plan}
73	
74	        Current plan metadata:
75	        {json_dump(latest_meta).strip()}
76	
77	        Gate summary:
78	        {json_dump(gate).strip()}
79	
80	        Open significant flags:
81	        {json_dump(open_flags).strip()}
82	
83	        {settled_block}
84	
85	        Requirements:
86	        - Each open flag is a committed generative proposal from a lens persona. Treat them as *menu*, not *checklist*.
87	        - The primary criterion for this scene is **{primary_criterion}**. This is your through-line.
88	        - You MAY and SHOULD reject a flag when: (a) it fights the primary criterion, (b) it fights another stronger flag you are keeping, (c) integrating it would require neutering the scene's existing weirdness. Record rejections in `flags_addressed` as `{{"id": "FLAG-X", "resolution": "rejected", "reason": "..."}}`.
89	        - Select and integrate only the flags that strengthen the scene's coherence under the primary criterion. If a stronger kept flag makes a weaker one redundant, reject the weaker one explicitly instead of sanding both down.
90	        - Your output is a revised **scene canvas** (premise, tone anchor, characters, objective, obstacle, turn, button), not a task list.
91	        - Do NOT address every flag. The harness will not measure you against flag-coverage; it will measure the final scene against the primary criterion.
92	        - Return `flags_addressed` with the exact flag IDs or rejection records you handled. Addressed flags may remain bare IDs; rejected flags must use the object form with `id`, `resolution`, and `reason`.
93	        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision and what you intentionally rejected.
94	        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`.
95	        - Verify that the revised scene canvas remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal weirdness.
96	        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised scene canvas markdown must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
97	        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.
98	
99	        Use this canvas shape inside the `plan` field:
100	
101	        # Scene Canvas: [Title]
102	        ## Overview
103	        [One short paragraph describing the comic engine and why it serves the primary criterion.]
104	        ## Premise
105	        [...]
106	        ## Tone Anchor
107	        [...]
108	        ## Characters
109	        [...]
110	        ## Objective
111	        [...]
112	        ## Obstacle
113	        [...]
114	        ## Turn
115	        [...]
116	        ## Button
117	        [...]
118	        """
119	    ).strip()
120
```

```
118	def _execute_batch_prompt(
119	    state: PlanState,
120	    plan_dir: Path,
121	    batch_task_ids: list[str],
122	    completed_task_ids: set[str] | None = None,
123	    root: Path | None = None,
124	) -> str:

126	    if mode == "doc":

128	    if mode == "joke":
129	        return _execute_joke_batch_prompt(state, plan_dir, batch_task_ids, completed_task_ids, root=root)
130	    return _execute_code_batch_prompt(state, plan_dir, batch_task_ids, completed_task_ids, root=root)

133	def _resolve_builder(
134	    builders: dict[str, _PromptBuilder], step: str, state: PlanState, agent_label: str
135	) -> _PromptBuilder:
136	    mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
137	    if mode == "joke":
138	        if step == "prep":
139	            return _prep_joke_prompt
140	        if step == "critique":

150	                criteria_guidance="Judge first against the declared primary criterion, then against the remaining success criteria and scene-canvas commitments.",

152	                sense_check_guidance="Review every sense check explicitly. Confirm concise executor acknowledgments when they are specific; dig deeper only when they are perfunctory or contradicted by the scene text.",
153	            )
154	    if mode == "doc":
155	        if step == "prep":
156	            return _prep_doc_prompt
157	        if step == "execute":

165	                sense_check_guidance="Review every sense check explicitly. Confirm concise executor acknowledgments when they are specific; dig deeper only when they are perfunctory or contradicted by the document.",
166	            )

168	    if builder is None:
```

> AGENT

Plan submitted. Summary of the design choices baked into it:

- **Forms registry** (`megaplan/forms/`) holds form-specific provocations, beat IDs, and provocateur voices — prompt files stay shared.
- **Provocation engine** replaces the static joke "lens" array; selector enforces 1-cut/1-force/1-spark default and disjoint provocations across iterations.
- **Stance** is structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`) with a soft validator that bubbles violations into a stance sub-provocation rather than hard-rejecting the run.
- **Stop-affordance** rides on the existing task_update via a `stop_signal` flag — no new outcome status, so the existing `done|skipped|blocked` enum stays intact.
- **Director's notes** as a JSON sidecar at `plan_dir/directors_notes.json`, plus stance also stamped into per-task receipts.
- **Joke** stays as both an alias (`--mode joke`) and a registered form (`--mode creative --form joke`); existing joke-mode plans keep dispatching through the same code paths.
- **Poem** is the second validation form (different beats — `opening_image / turn / close` — so it stresses the abstraction).
- **Robustness** maps to *different angles and rotating provocateur voices*, not more cycles; superrobust collapses to robust to honor the brief's "don't grind into mush" principle.

Six clarifying questions remain on the design branches — most importantly whether you want a markdown rendering of director's notes, whether stance should be structured vs free-form, and whether poem is the right second form vs ad copy.

        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar**, add an executor **stop-affordance**, and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

Current shape:
- Mode selection at `megaplan/cli.py:885`; init validation at `megaplan/handlers/init.py:48-72` (writes `state.config.mode`, `output_path`, `primary_criterion`).
- Prompt dispatch by `state.config.mode` at `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:118-170` (string-equality on `"joke"`).
- Joke prompts: `prep_joke.py`, `critique_joke.py`, `revise_joke.py`, `execute_joke.py`, `review_joke.py`.
- Static joke critique lenses: `megaplan/audits/robustness.py:140-217` (`JOKE_CRITIQUE_CHECKS`, `joke_checks_for_robustness`).
- Schemas: `megaplan/schemas.py` (`finalize.json`, `execution.json`); finalize task `status` enum is `pending|done|skipped|blocked`; execution `task_updates.status` is `done|skipped|blocked`.
- Tests: `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`, `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`, plus `tests/bakeoff/*` (currently modified on this branch — leave those alone unless impacted).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

Form-specific content (lenses, prep checklists, beat IDs) lives **inside the forms registry**; prompt builders read from a single `Form` object so we don't fork five new prompt files per form. The shared scaffolds in `prep_joke.py`/`critique_joke.py`/etc. become `prep_creative.py`/etc., parameterized by the active `Form`. The existing files stay as thin shims (or are renamed and re-exported) so the prompt-dispatch import surface keeps working.

Joke-mode plans created before this lands keep working unchanged: `state.config.mode == "joke"` continues to dispatch through the same code paths. New plans set `mode = "creative"` and `form = "joke"|"poem"`. The CLI accepts both forms (joke is an alias).

## Phase 1: Foundation — forms registry, mode plumbing, schemas

### Step 1: Create the forms registry (`megaplan/forms/__init__.py`, `megaplan/forms/joke.py`, `megaplan/forms/poem.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/__init__.py` exporting:
   - `Form` dataclass with fields: `id: str`, `display_name: str`, `output_extension: str`, `beat_ids: tuple[str, ...]`, `prep_checklist: tuple[str, ...]` (form-specific brief items), `provocations: ProvocationCatalog`, `stance_voice_hint: str`, `provocateur_voices: tuple[ProvocateurVoice, ...]`.
   - `ProvocationCatalog` dataclass with `cuts: tuple[Provocation, ...]`, `forces: tuple[Provocation, ...]`, `sparks: tuple[Provocation, ...]`.
   - `Provocation` dataclass with `id: str`, `vector: Literal["cut","force","spark"]`, `subtype: str` (e.g. `"hostile_gift"`, `"halve"`, `"darling"`), `prompt_text: str`, `targets: tuple[str, ...]` (draft-state tags this provocation is meant for: `"over_explained"`, `"safe"`, `"bloated"`, `"replaceable"`, `"any"`).
   - `ProvocateurVoice` dataclass with `id: str` (e.g. `"formalist"`, `"iconoclast"`, `"audience"`), `persona_text: str`, `vector_bias: tuple[str, ...]`.
   - `FORMS: dict[str, Form]` registry, `register(form)`, `get_form(id)`.

2. **Add** `megaplan/forms/joke.py` populating a `Form(id="joke", ...)` with the existing 9 joke lenses migrated to provocations. Each existing lens becomes a `Provocation` with `vector="spark"` and a `subtype` from the brief's spark sub-types (e.g. `subtext_inversion → spark/inversion`, `genre_swap → spark/forced_transplant`). Add ~3 cuts ("cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep", "kill the explanation", "strip the laugh-track beat"), ~3 forces ("halve it", "no character speaks", "must rhyme by end of beat"). Beat IDs: `("opening","inciting","obstacle","turn","button")`. Provocateur voices: `formalist`, `iconoclast`, `audience` with persona text in this file.

3. **Add** `megaplan/forms/poem.py` — the second validation form (per brief: "cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)"). Beat IDs: `("opening_image","turn","close")`. Reuse vector definitions but with poem-specific prompt text (e.g. force "halve it. write the line that survives.", spark "borrow one move from a master poet, named"). Smaller catalog is fine — three of each vector minimum so the firing pattern works.

4. **Register** both forms at `megaplan/forms/__init__.py` import time.

### Step 2: Generalize mode/form CLI surface (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** `megaplan/cli.py:885-899` to add `creative` to `--mode` choices and add `--form` argument (choices come from `megaplan.forms.FORMS.keys()`). Help text: `"Creative work form (e.g. joke, poem). Required with --mode creative."`.
2. **Update** `megaplan/handlers/init.py:48-72`:
   - Accept `mode == "joke"` and treat it as `mode = "creative"`, `form = "joke"` (alias). Same for any future single-form aliases.
   - When `mode == "creative"`, require `--form` and validate against `FORMS`; require `--output`; allow `--primary-criterion`.
   - Persist `state["config"]["form"] = form` alongside `mode`.
3. **Resolve design branch #6 (default):** `--primary-criterion` becomes valid for all `--mode creative` (any form), not joke-only. Update the existing `if raw_primary_criterion and mode != "joke"` check at `megaplan/handlers/init.py:54` to allow it for any creative mode.
4. **Backward compat:** plans whose state already has `mode == "joke"` (no `form` field) keep working. The dispatcher (Step 4) treats missing `form` as `"joke"` when `mode == "joke"`.

### Step 3: Extend schemas for stance, sidecar, stop-affordance (`megaplan/schemas.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** `execution.json` `task_updates[]` (line 357) to add optional fields:
   - `stance: { challenge_engaged: string, angle_taken: string, what_changed: string }` (resolves design branch #1 → structured; cheaper to validate the ≤50-word and "no hedging verbs" rule on a single composed string).
   - `stop_signal: { requested: bool, defense: string }` (resolves design branch #3 → stance flag, not new outcome status; keeps the existing `done|skipped|blocked` enum untouched).

3. **Add** a new artifact entry `directors_notes.json` with shape:
   ```json
   {
     "form": "joke",
     "primary_criterion": "...",
     "passes": [
       {
         "iteration": 1,
   ```

## Phase 2: Provocation engine + stance enforcement

### Step 4: Build provocation selection logic (`megaplan/forms/provocations.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `select_provocations(form: Form, *, robustness: str, iteration: int, draft_state_tags: tuple[str, ...], prior_provocation_ids: set[str]) -> tuple[Provocation, ...]`.
2. **Default firing pattern (resolves design branch #5 → formal table within bounds):**
   - `light`: `(1 cut, 1 spark)` — skip the force per brief.
   - `standard`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` on prep, then on critique iteration N>1 fire a *different* triad biased away from `prior_provocation_ids`.
   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, but each cycle uses a different `ProvocateurVoice` from `form.provocateur_voices`. Voice rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience → ...`. Resolves design branch #4 → fixed roster (deterministic; auditable; honors brief's named voices).
3. **Targeting:** prefer provocations whose `targets` overlap with `draft_state_tags`. Deterministic tie-break by `id`. If no match, fall back to `targets == ("any",)`.
4. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None` (returns None for light/standard).
5. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown to tag `over_explained` (long prose-to-beat ratio), `safe` (no flagged adjectives like "weird", "risky"), `bloated` (>N words), `replaceable` (no named characters/specifics). Keep this dumb-but-deterministic; the executor still chooses how to engage.

### Step 5: Rewrite critique + revise as the provocation engine (`megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`:
   - Replaces the lens-based `_critique_joke_prompt`. Takes `form` from registry.
   - Calls `select_provocations(...)` and `select_provocateur_voice(...)`.
   - Renders a prompt that names: the chosen voice persona, exactly the (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) targeted at the current draft state, and instructions to **commit** to one concrete proposal per provocation (no hedging, no menus).
   - Standard rule: critique iteration must fire *different* provocations from the prior pass — pass `prior_provocation_ids` from the previous iteration's `directors_notes.json` entry.
   - The output template follows the existing `findings`/`flags` shape so `flag_registry` still works — no schema rewrite needed.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`:
   - Mirrors `revise_joke.py` but reads provocation IDs out of the flags rather than lens IDs. Same canvas shape (premise, tone, characters, etc.) but **driven by `form.beat_ids`** (so poem mode renders `opening_image / turn / close`, not screenwriting beats).
3. **Update** `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`:
   - Keep `joke_checks_for_robustness` exporting (back-compat) but reimplement it to call into the form registry: `joke_checks_for_robustness = lambda r: form_provocations_as_checks(get_form("joke"), r)`.
   - Add `form_provocations_as_checks(form, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]` that maps the selected provocations to the existing `CritiqueCheckSpec` shape so downstream validation (`validate_critique_checks`) keeps working unchanged.

   - Add a `_resolve_form(state)` helper: returns `get_form(state.config.form)` if `mode == "creative"`, else `get_form("joke")` if `mode == "joke"` (alias), else `None`.
   - Replace the `if mode == "joke"` block with `if mode in {"creative", "joke"}` and route through the new `*_creative.py` builders, passing `form` in.
   - Keep `_prep_joke_prompt`, `_critique_joke_prompt`, etc. as thin wrappers that call the new creative versions with `form=get_form("joke")` so the existing imports / `__all__` still resolve.

### Step 6: Stance field in execute prompts + validator (`megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py`, new `megaplan/forms/stance.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/stance.py`:
   - `STANCE_HEDGING_VERBS = ("considered","tried","attempted","sought to","aimed to","worked to")`.
   - `validate_stance(stance: dict) -> list[str]` returning a list of violations:
     - missing `challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`,
     - composed string > 50 words,
     - first-person check: composed string lacks any of `("i ","i'm","i've","my ")`,
     - hedging-verb hit,
     - position check: heuristic — must contain at least one of a configurable set of "claim" markers (e.g. `"because"`, `"refused"`, `"chose"`, `"killed"`) OR be flagged for human review.
   - Pure function, no I/O. The harness enforces it post-hoc and bubbles violations into a stance sub-provocation on the next pass (per brief: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too.").

2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`:
   - Generalizes `execute_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` for `sections_written`. Reads `form.stance_voice_hint` to seed the stance prompt.
   - Adds explicit stance instructions to the requirements block: shape, ≤50 words, first person, names the specific provocation engaged, no hedging verbs, must take a position someone could disagree with.
   - Adds stop-affordance instructions: executor MAY set `stop_signal.requested = true` with a `defense` (1 sentence) explaining why the next pass would damage the piece. Code mode never sets this.
3. **Refactor** `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py` to a 5-line shim: `_execute_joke_prompt = partial(_execute_creative_prompt, form=get_form("joke"))`, same for batch. Keep `__all__` so the prompts package re-export still works.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:144-145` to dispatch to `_execute_creative_prompt` for `mode == "creative"`.

### Step 7: Honor stop-affordance + write directors-notes sidecar (`megaplan/_core/io.py` or wherever execution result is consumed; `megaplan/handlers/`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Audit** where `execution.json` is consumed and the next critique/execute cycle is decided. (Likely in the auto driver / orchestrator — search for the place that increments iteration after review and decides whether to run another critique/revise cycle.) Add a check: when `mode == "creative"` AND any `task_updates[].stop_signal.requested == true` AND review verdict is `approved`, halt further critique/revise cycles even if robustness budget remains. Record the halt + defense text in a new `meta.notes` entry and in `directors_notes.json`.
2. **Add** a small assembler in `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:
   - `assemble_directors_notes(plan_dir, state) -> dict`: walks the plan dir for per-iteration critique flags + finalize task stances + execute stop signals, builds the schema from Step 3.
   - Called once at the end of each execute and at finalize; writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json`.
3. **Add** stance validation enforcement: when the harness ingests `execution.json`, run `validate_stance` on each `task_updates[].stance`. Violations are logged to `meta.notes` and surface as a `flags` entry on the next critique pass tagged `"stance_authenticity"`. Do NOT hard-reject the execution — soft enforcement so we don't lose the work.

### Step 11: End-to-end smoke (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** smoke test mirroring `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`:
   - Init `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md`, run prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review with mocked agents that produce minimal valid JSON (including a valid stance).
   - Assert `directors_notes.json` is written with at least one stance entry and one fired provocation.
   - Assert stop-signal halts further cycles when set.

### Step 12: Run focused tests, then full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_forms_registry.py tests/test_provocations.py tests/test_stance_validator.py -x` first (cheapest validation).
2. **Run** `pytest tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py tests/test_prompts_creative.py -x` next.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py -x` after the wiring is in place.
4. **Run** the full `pytest tests/` to catch any regression in unrelated tests (especially `tests/bakeoff/*`, which is currently dirty on this branch but should be unaffected by mode work).

---

## Execution Order

1. Phase 1 (forms registry, mode/form plumbing, schemas) before any prompt rewrite — every later phase depends on the registry existing.
2. Within Phase 1: Step 1 (forms package) → Step 2 (CLI/init) → Step 3 (schemas), in that order.
3. Phase 2: Step 4 (selection logic) → Step 5 (critique/revise rewrite) → Step 6 (execute + stance) → Step 7 (sidecar + stop-affordance honoring). Steps 5 and 6 each depend on Step 4.
4. Phase 3 tests can begin in parallel with Step 5 once Steps 1-4 are committed (registry + selection are pure-functional and testable on their own).
5. The joke→creative shim in Step 5 is the riskiest moment for back-compat — land it with both `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py` and `tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py` green before moving on.

## Validation Order

1. Pure-function unit tests first (registry, selection, stance validator) — no fixtures, no plan dir.
2. Init/CLI tests next — fast, isolated.
3. Prompt builder tests — fixture-based but no agent calls.
4. End-to-end smoke tests with mocked agents.
5. Full pytest suite to catch regressions in adjacent code (especially the in-flight bakeoff changes on this branch).

Stop-affordance is also misplaced \u2014 gating halt on approved reviews is a no-op because approved reviews already return STATE_DONE; the damaging-pass scenario lives on the rework path.   "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "id": "branch-1-stance-shape",
      "decision": "Stance is structured: {challenge_engaged, angle_taken, what_changed}.",
      "rationale": "Field-by-field validation (\u226450 words composed, no hedging verbs, names the provocation) is enforceable on a structured shape; free-form string would force regex-only checks. Plan documents this; no critique flag disputed it."
    },
    {
      "id": "branch-2-directors-notes-destination",
      "decision": "Separate JSON sidecar at plan_dir/directors_notes.json plus stance fields in receipts (no markdown rendering for now).",
      "rationale": "Machine-parseable artifact stands alone per brief; receipts carry per-task stance for traceability. Markdown rendering can be a follow-up."
    },
    {
      "id": "branch-3-stop-affordance-shape",
      "decision": "Stance flag on the existing task_update (no new outcome status, no halt-task type).",
      "rationale": "Composes with existing `done|skipped|blocked` enum without state-machine changes. Note: the *check point* for the flag still needs to move \u2014 see warnings."
    },
    {
      "id": "branch-4-provocateur-voices",
      "decision": "Fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) rotated deterministically by iteration.",
      "rationale": "Auditable, reproducible, honors brief's named voices. Model-chosen-per-cycle can be a config opt-in later."
    },
    {
      "id": "branch-5-robustness-mapping",
      "decision": "Formal table within bounds: light=(1 cut, 1 spark), standard=(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark), robust=triad per cycle with rotating voice; superrobust \u2192 robust (no extra cycles, brief warns against more cycles).",
      "rationale": "Brief is explicit on counts. Tying robustness to angle-diversity rather than cycle-count honors the over-process guard."
    },
    {
      "id": "branch-6-primary-criterion-scope",
      "decision": "Lift `--primary-criterion` to all `--mode creative` (any form), not joke-only.",
      "rationale": "Brief frames primary criterion as load-bearing for any creative work; treating it as joke-specific would block poem and future forms from the same affordance."
    },
    {
      "id": "second-form-poem",
      "decision": "Poem (not ad copy) is the second validation form.",
      "rationale": "Different beat IDs (opening_image / turn / close) and different voice hint stress the abstraction harder than ad copy, which is closer to joke's commercial register."
    },
    {
      "id": "joke-alias-preserved",
      "decision": "`--mode joke` remains a CLI alias for `--mode creative --form joke`; pre-v0.21 state files with `mode='joke'` and no `form` are treated as creative+joke at dispatch.",
      "rationale": "Brief explicitly requires this; convenience for in-flight plans."
    },
    {
      "id": "stance-validator-soft-enforcement",
      "decision": "Stance validator is soft-enforced \u2014 violations log to meta.notes and bubble into the next critique as a stance-authenticity sub-provocation, not hard-rejected.",
      "rationale": "Hard rejection forces re-runs for hedging-verb violations, contradicting the brief's 'don't grind into nervous mush' principle."
    }
  ],
