  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.\n\n## The provocation engine\n\nThe brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor \u2014 adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:\n\n- **Cuts** \u2014 strip / expose / kill. Example: \"Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling.\"\n- **Forces** \u2014 perform a transformation on the piece. Example: \"Halve it. Write the line that survives.\"\n- **Sparks** \u2014 the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:\n  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)\n  - Substitutions (\"what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene\")\n  - Inversions (\"the version of this that's a confession, not a performance\")\n  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)\n  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)\n  - Hostile claims (\"this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about\" \u2014 refute or absorb)\n  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)\n  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)\n  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)\n\nDefault firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state \u2014 over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + \"who could have made this.\"\n\n## The stance\n\nEvery executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:\n\n- \u226450 words\n- First person\n- Names the specific provocation engaged\n- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)\n- Must take a position someone could disagree with\n\nA sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: \"Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger.\"\n\nStance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a \"director's notes\" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece \u2014 the artifact must stand alone.\n\n## Robustness mapping\n\nCreative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* \u2014 not just more cycles.\n\n- **Light** \u2014 single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance \u22642 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.\n- **Standard** \u2014 one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.\n- **Robust** \u2014 multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.\n\n## Executor stop-affordance\n\nLet the maker signal \"the next pass would damage the piece\" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.\n\n## Generalize joke \u2192 creative-work\n\n- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form\n- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` \u2014 no breakage of existing plans\n- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)\n- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form\n\n## Design branches the prep phase should resolve\n\nThe following are real branches with arguments either way \u2014 settle them in prep, not in code:\n\n1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)\n2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts\n3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type\n4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle\n5. Robustness \u2192 provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds\n6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- New forms beyond the second validation form\n- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)\n- Team-of-makers workflows\n- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)\n\n## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)\n\nSharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something \u2014 and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is \"more rigorous\" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece \u2014 it is what converts the system from \"external pressure\" into \"training the maker as their own provocateur.\"",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

210	def _synthesize_review_rework_items(checks: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
211	    rework_items: list[dict[str, str]] = []
212	    for check in checks:
213	        check_id = check.get("id", "")
214	        if not isinstance(check_id, str) or not check_id:
215	            continue
216	        check_def = review_checks.get_check_by_id(check_id)
217	        if getattr(check_def, "default_severity", "") != "likely-significant":
218	            continue
219	        question = str(check.get("question", "") or "").strip()
220	        findings = check.get("findings", [])
221	        if not isinstance(findings, list):
222	            continue
223	        for finding in findings:
224	            if not isinstance(finding, dict) or not finding.get("flagged"):
225	                continue
226	            status = str(finding.get("status", "") or "").strip().lower()
227	            # megaplan/prompts/review.py:243-249 constrains status to {blocking, significant, minor, n/a};
228	            # significant is the explicit non-blocking downgrade for gate-settled concerns, while missing or
229	            # empty status means the model failed to classify, so we keep the check's default_severity gate as
230	            # the blocking fallback. That is the safe default for the sympy-21930 / sphinx-9711 regressions.
231	            if status and status != "blocking":
232	                continue
233	            detail = str(finding.get("detail", "") or "").strip()
234	            evidence_file = finding.get("evidence_file", "")
235	            if not isinstance(evidence_file, str):
236	                evidence_file = ""
237	            issue = detail or question or f"Heavy review found a blocking {check_id} issue."
238	            # Prefer a per-check actionable expected string; fall back to the
239	            # check's self-question; ultimately fall back to a generic message.
240	            expected = (
241	                _EXPECTED_BY_CHECK_ID.get(check_id)
242	                or question
243	                or f"Review check '{check_id}' should pass without blocking findings."
244	            )
245	            # `actual` should NOT duplicate `issue` — use a templated
246	            # acknowledgment of the finding instead so the executor sees
247	            # a clear "you didn't resolve it" signal without a copy of
248	            # the detail text.
249	            actual = f"The diff did not resolve the flagged {check_id} concern above."
250	            rework_items.append(
251	                {
252	                    "task_id": f"REVIEW-{check_id}",
253	                    "issue": issue,
254	                    "expected": expected,
255	                    "actual": actual,
256	                    "evidence_file": evidence_file,
257	                    "source": f"review_{check_id}",
258	                }
259	            )
260	    return rework_items

662	def strict_schema(schema: Any, _path: tuple[str, ...] = ()) -> Any:

664	        updated = {key: strict_schema(value, _path + (key,)) for key, value in schema.items()}
665	        preserve_explicit_required = bool(updated.pop("x-preserve-explicit-required", False))
666	        if updated.get("type") == "object":
667	            updated.setdefault("additionalProperties", False)
668	            if "properties" in updated and not (
669	                preserve_explicit_required or _preserve_explicit_required(_path)
670	            ):
671	                updated["required"] = list(updated["properties"].keys())
672	        return updated
673	    if isinstance(schema, list):
674	        return [strict_schema(item, _path) for item in schema]
675	    return schema
676

> AGENT

The critique is correct — none of the flags suggest the plan targets the wrong code. They're all completeness gaps where the plan addressed prompts/init but missed the broader mode-aware surface area, the workflow override that skips prep on standard, and put the stop-affordance check at the wrong vertex. The right fix is a single-point-of-truth `is_creative_mode()` / `creative_form_id()` helper that every `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` site delegates to, plus moving provocation selection out of prep-only (so standard, which skips prep, still gets the triad at critique time), plus moving the stop-affordance check to the rework branch in `_resolve_review_outcome()`, plus integrating director's notes at the merge boundary instead of crawling artifacts.

On critique iteration N>1, exclude `prior_provocation_ids`.\n   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, biased by the cycle's voice's `vector_bias`.\n2. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None`: rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience` for `robust`/`superrobust`; returns `None` for light/standard. **Resolves branch #4.**\n3. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown. Tag `over_explained`, `safe`, `bloated`, `replaceable`. Pure, deterministic.\n4. **Add** `select_active_checks(state, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]`: the dispatcher that wraps `select_provocations` for the active form (or falls back to `checks_for_robustness(robustness)` when `not is_creative_mode(state)`). Reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json` if it exists. This single function is what `handle_critique`, the Hermes template rewriter, and the worker mocks all call.\n\n### Step 9: Critique + revise prompt builders (`megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n\n1. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`:\n   - Reads `form = get_form(creative_form_id(state))`.\n   - Calls `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` (same call the harness makes elsewhere — single source of truth).\n   - Renders the chosen voice persona, the (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) named, and instructs commit-to-one-concrete-proposal-per-provocation.\n   - On standard iteration N>1: prompt explicitly forbids relitigating prior provocation IDs (read from `directors_notes.json`).\n   - Output template uses the existing `findings`/`flags` shape; each fired provocation becomes a `FLAG-<provocation-id>`.\n2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`:\n   - Mirrors `revise_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` to render the canvas (poem renders `opening_image / turn / close`, joke keeps `opening / inciting / obstacle / turn / button`). **Resolves \"Prompt builders for poem mode reference poem beat IDs.\"**\n   - The standard-mode revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance (per brief). Add an instruction block referencing the prior `stance` from the previous iteration's finalize.\n3. **Update** `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`:\n   - Reimplement `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` to call `select_active_checks` with a synthetic creative-joke state. Mark deprecated in a docstring; existing callers keep working.\n   - Add `creative_checks_for_robustness(form, robustness)` exported alongside.\n4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py`: route `is_creative_mode(state)` through the creative builders; keep the joke shims.\n\n### Step 10: Stance validator + execute prompt builder (`megaplan/forms/stance.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n\n1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/stance.py`:\n   - `STANCE_HEDGING_VERBS = (\"considered\",\"tried\",\"attempted\",\"sought to\",\"aimed to\",\"worked to\")`.\n   - `STANCE_CLAIM_MARKERS = (\"because\",\"refused\",\"chose\",\"killed\",\"kept\",\"fought\",\"insisted\",\"picked\")`.\n   - `validate_stance(stance: dict) -> list[str]` — returns violations (missing field, >50 words composed, missing first-person marker, hedging hit, no claim marker). Pure function.\n2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`:\n   - Generalizes `execute_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` for `sections_written`; reads `form.stance_voice_hint`.\n   - Stance instructions in requirements block: shape, ≤50 words composed, first person, names the specific provocation engaged, no hedging verbs, must take a position.\n   - Stop-affordance instructions: executor MAY set `stop_signal.requested=true` with a `defense` (1 sentence) explaining why the next pass would damage the piece. Code mode never sets this. **Resolves \"Execute prompt for code mode does NOT include stop-affordance.\"**\n3. **Refactor** `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py` to a shim: `_execute_joke_prompt = partial(_execute_creative_prompt, form=get_form(\"joke\"))`, same for batch. Re-export through `__all__`.\n4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128` to dispatch to `_execute_creative_prompt` for `is_creative_mode(state)`.\n\n### Step 11: Stop-affordance at the rework branch (`megaplan/handlers/review.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Audit** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201`. The damaging extra pass happens at line 191 (`return \"needs_rework\", STATE_FINALIZED, \"execute\"`).\n2. **Add** stop-signal honoring on the rework branch only:\n   ```python\n   if rework_requested and is_creative_mode(state) and _maker_requested_stop(plan_dir):\n       _record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense=_collect_stop_defenses(plan_dir))\n       return \"success\", STATE_DONE, None\n   ```\n   Place this check between the existing `rework_requested` block and the cap-key lookup so a maker stop short-circuits before the rework cycle counter increments.\n3. **Add** helpers:\n   - `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)`: reads the latest `finalize.json` and returns True if any task has `stance.stop_signal.requested == true`.\n   - `_collect_stop_defenses(plan_dir)`: returns the joined `stop_signal.defense` strings.\n   - `_record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense)`: writes a `meta.notes` entry and updates `directors_notes.json`.\n4. **Resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2.**\n\n### Step 12: Director's notes assembled at merge boundary (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:\n   - `update_directors_notes_from_merge(plan_dir, state, merged_payload, *, iteration, voice, fired_provocations) -> None`: appends a `passes[]` entry derived from the merged payload's `task_updates[].stance` and `stop_signal` fields. Reads/writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json` atomically.\n2. **Hook** into `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`) and `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`: when `is_creative_mode(state)`, call `update_directors_notes_from_merge(...)` with the merged payload. The fired-provocation IDs come from the latest `critique_v<N>.json` `flags` array filtered by `FLAG-` prefix.\n3. **Hook** into `handle_critique()` so the *initial* triad is recorded in `directors_notes.json` immediately after critique writes, even when execution hasn't run yet (so iteration N+1's critique can read prior provocation IDs).\n4. **Resolves scope-2** (sidecar at merge boundary, not crawler).\n\n### Step 13: Stance validation enforcement (`megaplan/execute/core.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Update** the merge step (`megaplan/execute/core.py:_merge_batch_results`) to call `validate_stance(task_update.stance)` for each task update with a stance. Violations are stored on the task as `stance_violations: list[str]` (added to `directors_notes.json`'s `stances[]` entries).\n2. **Bubble** violations into the next critique pass: the critique prompt builder (Step 9) reads `directors_notes.json` and, if the prior pass had `stance_violations`, prepends a stance-authenticity sub-provocation per the brief: \"Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger.\"\n3. **Soft enforcement only** (no hard reject — preserves the brief's \"don't grind into nervous mush\" principle).\n4. **Resolves \"Stance authenticity check fires as sub-provocation.\"**\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4: Tests + validation\n\n### Step 14: Pure-function unit tests (`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`, `tests/test_forms_registry.py`, `tests/test_provocations.py`, `tests/test_stance_validator.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`**: `is_creative_mode` true for `mode=creative` and `mode=joke`; `creative_form_id` returns `\"joke\"` for legacy joke state with no form key, returns `state.config.form` for new state, returns `None` for code mode. `is_prose_mode` true for doc/joke/creative.\n2. **`tests/test_forms_registry.py`**: `get_form(\"joke\")` and `get_form(\"poem\")` exist; both have ≥3 cuts/forces/sparks; joke has 5 beat IDs, poem has 3; both declare `execution_schema_key == \"execution_doc.json\"`.\n3. **`tests/test_provocations.py`**: light=2, standard=3, robust=3 with rotating voice; `prior_provocation_ids` filters duplicates on standard iteration 2; `over_explained` tag biases selection.\n4. **`tests/test_stance_validator.py`**: valid passes; >50 words → violation; hedging verb → violation; third-person → violation; no claim marker → violation.\n\n### Step 15: Workflow + handler integration tests (`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`, `tests/test_workflow_creative.py`, `tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`**: `--mode creative --form joke --output …` succeeds and writes `state.config.form=\"joke\"`; `--mode creative` without `--form` raises `invalid_args`; `--mode creative --form poem --primary-criterion \"tightest image\"` accepted.\n2. **Update** `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`: `--mode joke` still works AND now writes `state.config.form=\"joke\"`.\n3. **`tests/test_workflow_creative.py`**: build a synthetic creative-mode state at `STATE_INITIALIZED` with `robustness=standard`; assert `workflow_next()` returns `prep` (not `plan`). Same for joke alias. Code-mode standard still returns `plan`.\n4. **`tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`**: build a state with a needs_rework review and a `stop_signal.requested=true` task in finalize; assert `_resolve_review_outcome()` returns `(\"success\", STATE_DONE, None)` and `meta.notes` records the defense. Without the stop signal, the same input returns `(\"needs_rework\", STATE_FINALIZED, \"execute\")` (existing behavior preserved). Code mode with stop_signal does NOT short-circuit (helper returns False).\n\n### Step 16: Schema + runtime-layout tests (`tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** `tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`: call `ensure_runtime_layout()`; assert `.megaplan/schemas/execution_doc.json` exists and includes `stance` + `stop_signal` properties; assert `.megaplan/schemas/directors_notes.json` exists; assert `get_execution_schema_key(\"creative\", \"poem\") == \"execution_doc.json\"`.\n\n### Step 17: Prompt builder tests (`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`**:\n   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=joke, robustness=\"standard\")` mentions exactly 1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark by name.\n   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=poem, ...)` references poem beats, not screenplay beats.\n   - `_execute_creative_prompt` includes stance + stop-affordance instructions.\n   - Code-mode `_execute_prompt` does NOT include stop-affordance instructions.\n   - Iteration 2 critique excludes provocation IDs from a fixture `directors_notes.json`.\n   - Dispatcher: `create_claude_prompt(\"critique\", state_with_mode_joke, …)` ≡ `create_claude_prompt(\"critique\", state_with_mode_creative_form_joke, …)`.\n   - `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` returns the same output as `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` for joke (back-compat).\n\n### Step 18: End-to-end smoke (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Add** smoke test with mocked agents:\n   - `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md` → prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review.\n   - Mocked execute returns a valid stance + `stop_signal.requested=false`. Assert `directors_notes.json` is written via the merge boundary, contains form, primary_criterion, fired provocations, stances.\n   - Second run: mocked execute returns `stop_signal.requested=true`, mocked review returns `needs_rework`. Assert review halts with STATE_DONE and `meta.notes` records the defense.\n\n### Step 19: Run focused tests, then full suite\n**Scope:** Small\n\n1. **Run** unit tests (Steps 14, 16) — fastest, no fixtures.\n2. **Run** init/workflow/review tests (Step 15).\n3. **Run** prompt builder tests (Step 17).\n4. **Run** smoke (Step 18) and existing `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`.\n5. **Run** full `pytest tests/` to catch regressions, especially anywhere a `mode in {\"doc\", \"joke\"}` site was rewritten.\n\n---\n\n## Execution Order\n\n1. **Phase 1** (helpers + registry + schemas + workflow) lands first. Every later phase depends on the helpers existing.\n2. Inside Phase 1: Step 1 (helpers) → Step 2 (forms registry) → Step 3 (init/CLI) → Step 4 (workflow override) → Step 5 (schemas + runtime layout). Steps 1 and 2 can land in either order; Step 3 depends on both.\n3. **Phase 2** (mode-aware sweep) is mechanical and runs after Phase 1. Each replaced site gets its file-local pytest before moving on.\n4. **Phase 3** (provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance + sidecar) depends on Steps 1, 2, 6 being done. Step 8 (selection) → Step 9 (critique/revise prompts) → Step 10 (stance + execute prompt) → Step 11 (stop-affordance handler) → Step 12 (sidecar at merge) → Step 13 (stance validation enforcement).\n5. **Phase 4 tests** can begin in parallel with Phase 3 once Steps 1-8 are committed (all pure functions are testable on their own).\n6. The riskiest moment is Step 6 (mode sweep); land it green before starting Phase 3.\n\n## Validation Order\n\n1. Pure-function unit tests (helpers, registry, selection, stance validator) — no fixtures.\n2. Schema + runtime-layout tests — light I/O only.\n3. Workflow + handler tests — fixture-based but no agent calls.\n4. Prompt builder tests — fixture-based, deterministic.\n5. End-to-end smoke tests with mocked agents.\n6. Full pytest suite to catch regressions in adjacent code (especially `tests/bakeoff/*` and any file touched by the Phase 2 sweep).",
  "changes_summary": "Restructured into 4 phases: (1) added a single-source-of-truth `is_creative_mode(state)` / `creative_form_id(state)` / `is_prose_mode(state)` helper trio so every mode-aware site has one place to consult; (2) called out a mechanical sweep step naming all ~25 verified `mode in {\"doc\", \"joke\"}` and `mode == \"joke\"` sites by file:line and replacing each with the helper; (3) made the workflow override mode-aware so creative-standard runs prep (FLAG-001); (4) moved the stop-affordance check from the dead approved-review branch to the live needs_rework branch in `_resolve_review_outcome()` (FLAG-002); (5) integrated director's notes at the merge boundary inside `_merge_batch_results()` instead of crawling artifacts (scope-2); (6) declared per-form `execution_schema_key` so callers pass form, not just mode (callers-1, FLAG-003); (7) routed Hermes template rewriter, mock workers, and `handle_critique()` through a shared `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` dispatcher (correctness-2, callers-2); (8) added a runtime-layout test asserting the new schema entries reach `.megaplan/schemas/` (all_locations-1); (9) stamped `form` onto state at init time and added `creative_form_id()` so back-compat with legacy joke state files is contract-shaped, not ad-hoc (all_locations-2). Added two new test files and expanded smoke coverage to exercise the stop-affordance halt on rework. Success criteria expanded with explicit must-pass items for the workflow override, helper sweep, and stop-affordance vertex.",

  "assumptions": [
    "Legacy joke state (mode='joke', no form key) is handled by `creative_form_id()`'s fallback to 'joke', not by a one-time migration. The brief explicitly lifts the back-compat requirement for pre-v0.21 plan files, so this is convenience, not contract.",
    "Forms declare their own `execution_schema_key` (defaulting to `execution_doc.json`). Both joke and poem use `execution_doc.json` since `sections_written` works for any prose-with-beats form.",
    "The `--mode joke` value remains stored verbatim in state (not normalized to 'creative') so that any legacy code or external tooling string-equaling on it still works during the transition. The helpers treat both as creative.",
    "`select_provocations` reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json`; on iteration 1 (no prior file), the prior set is empty and selection is unconstrained.",
    "The mode-aware `_CREATIVE_OVERRIDES` for the workflow lives in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py` next to `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES`. Layering it via `workflow_next()` is less invasive than making `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES` mode-aware and lets code-mode behavior stay untouched.",
    "Stance validator stays soft-enforced: violations log + bubble into the next critique as a stance-authenticity sub-provocation rather than hard-reject. Hard reject would force re-runs for hedging-verb violations and contradict the brief's 'don't grind into nervous mush' principle.",
    "directors_notes.json is JSON only; markdown rendering is a follow-up if requested.",
    "Robust mode rotates a fixed roster (formalist/iconoclast/audience). Model-chosen voices can be a config opt-in later if needed.",
    "`--primary-criterion` is lifted to all `--mode creative` (any form), per the brief's framing of primary criterion as load-bearing for creative work.",
    "Poem (not ad copy) is the second validation form because it stresses the abstraction harder via different beat IDs and a different stance voice.",
    "Step 13's stance validation runs at merge time (so `directors_notes.json`'s `stance_violations` field is populated by the merge boundary, consistent with scope-2). The next critique reads it from the sidecar."
  ],

        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar** assembled at the merge boundary, add an executor **stop-affordance** at the correct vertex (the rework branch), and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

**Root-cause framing.** The previous draft fixed the prompt-builder layer but every other mode-aware surface (`mode in {"doc", "joke"}` and `mode == "joke"`) is duplicated across the codebase. Verified instances (from grep):
- `megaplan/evaluation.py:124`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py:64`, `megaplan/handlers/execute.py:106`, `megaplan/handlers/critique.py:120`, `megaplan/cli.py:307`, `megaplan/handlers/review.py:273`
- `megaplan/execute/core.py:163, 169, 209, 308, 401, 468, 1085`
- `megaplan/execute/timeout.py:49, 70, 138, 299`
- `megaplan/schemas.py:653` (`get_execution_schema_key`)
- `megaplan/workers.py:819`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:439`
- `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128, 137`, `megaplan/prompts/critique.py:302`, `megaplan/prompts/finalize.py:32`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py:103`, `megaplan/prompts/review.py:85`

These *all* answer one of two questions: "is this a prose-artifact mode?" or "which form's prompt builder do I use?" The fix is two helpers — `is_creative_mode(state) -> bool` and `creative_form_id(state) -> str | None` — added once, called everywhere. Same shape as the existing `configured_robustness(state)` helper. This is a mechanical sweep, not new logic.

**Workflow override (FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1).** `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES['standard']` at `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:90-94` maps `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skipping prep). The brief says standard creative mode fires the full triad on prep. Two ways to honor this: (a) branch the override on mode so creative-standard runs prep, or (b) move provocation *selection* out of prep so it runs at critique time too. We pick (b) for code mode (no change) and (a) for creative mode: provocation selection lives in a pure function callable from both prep and critique, and the workflow override is mode-aware so creative-standard runs prep (where the *initial* triad is selected and recorded), then critique re-selects against the prior triad. This is one workflow-override change + one selection-helper that doesn't care which step calls it.

**Stop-affordance at the correct vertex (FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2).** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201` already returns `STATE_DONE` for `approved` reviews. The damaging extra pass happens on the `needs_rework` branch (line 191). The check belongs on the rework branch: when `mode is creative` AND any `task_updates[].stop_signal.requested == true` AND `review_verdict == "needs_rework"`, treat it as a maker-honored halt — return `success`, `STATE_DONE` with the defense logged to `meta.notes` and `directors_notes.json`. Robust-mode pre-critique cycles also need a check, see Step 9.

**Director's notes at merge boundary (scope-2).** `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`) and `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` already normalize task updates into `finalize.json`. The director's-notes assembler hooks into those merge points, not a separate artifact crawler.

**Joke alias as a thin shim.** `--mode joke` continues to work. At init time it's stored as `mode = "joke"` (preserved verbatim for in-flight plans) AND `form = "joke"` is stamped onto config. The two helpers (`is_creative_mode`, `creative_form_id`) treat `mode in {"creative", "joke"}` as creative and resolve form from `state.config.form` (or `"joke"` when missing).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, `megaplan/_core/`, `megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/execute/timeout.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py`, `megaplan/evaluation.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

## Phase 1: Foundation — mode helpers, forms registry, schemas, workflow override

### Step 1: Add mode helpers (`megaplan/_core/modes.py`, re-export from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/_core/modes.py`:
   ```python
   def is_creative_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"creative", "joke"}

   def creative_form_id(state: PlanState) -> str | None:
       if not is_creative_mode(state):
           return None
       form = state.get("config", {}).get("form")
       if isinstance(form, str) and form.strip():
           return form.strip()
       # Back-compat: legacy joke plans have mode="joke", no form key.
       if state.get("config", {}).get("mode") == "joke":
           return "joke"
       return None

   def is_prose_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       """True for any mode that writes a prose artifact (doc, metaplan-as-doc, creative)."""
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"doc", "joke", "creative"}
   ```
2. **Re-export** from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py` so existing call sites can `from megaplan._core import is_creative_mode, creative_form_id, is_prose_mode`.
3. **Note for Step 6 sweep:** every `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` is logically `is_prose_mode(state)`. Every `mode == "joke"` is logically `is_creative_mode(state)` (joke today, joke+poem after this lands).

11. **`megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128, 137`**: replace `mode == "joke"` with `is_creative_mode(state)`; route through new `*_creative.py` builders, passing `form = get_form(creative_form_id(state))`. Keep the existing `_prep_joke_prompt`, `_critique_joke_prompt` etc. as thin shims (`partial(... , form=get_form("joke"))`).
12. **`megaplan/prompts/critique.py:302`**: same — `is_creative_mode(state)`.
13. **`megaplan/prompts/finalize.py:32`**: replace `plan_mode in {"doc", "joke"}` with `is_prose_mode(state)`.
14. **`megaplan/prompts/planning.py:103`**: replace `mode == "joke"` with `is_creative_mode(state)`. The branch may need to read the form's beat IDs to render the right canvas template.

For each replacement, run the file-local pytest before moving to the next file.

### Step 7: Update `get_execution_schema_key` callers (`megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py`, `megaplan/execute/core.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Audit** callers of `get_execution_schema_key()` at `megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290, 1395-1396` and `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283` and any in `megaplan/execute/`.
2. **Update** each caller to pass `form=creative_form_id(state)` alongside `mode`. Most callers already have `state` in scope. (Resolves callers-1.)

## Phase 3: Provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance

### Step 8: Provocation selection (`megaplan/forms/provocations.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `select_provocations(form, *, robustness, iteration, draft_state_tags, prior_provocation_ids) -> tuple[Provocation, ...]`. **Resolves branch #5 (formal table within bounds):**
   - `light`: `(1 cut, 1 spark)`.
   - `standard`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)`. On critique iteration N>1, exclude `prior_provocation_ids`.
   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, biased by the cycle's voice's `vector_bias`.
2. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None`: rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience` for `robust`/`superrobust`; returns `None` for light/standard. **Resolves branch #4.**
3. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown. Tag `over_explained`, `safe`, `bloated`, `replaceable`. Pure, deterministic.
4. **Add** `select_active_checks(state, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]`: the dispatcher that wraps `select_provocations` for the active form (or falls back to `checks_for_robustness(robustness)` when `not is_creative_mode(state)`). Reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json` if it exists. This single function is what `handle_critique`, the Hermes template rewriter, and the worker mocks all call.

### Step 9: Critique + revise prompt builders (`megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`:
   - Reads `form = get_form(creative_form_id(state))`.
   - Calls `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` (same call the harness makes elsewhere — single source of truth).
   - Renders the chosen voice persona, the (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) named, and instructs commit-to-one-concrete-proposal-per-provocation.
   - On standard iteration N>1: prompt explicitly forbids relitigating prior provocation IDs (read from `directors_notes.json`).
   - Output template uses the existing `findings`/`flags` shape; each fired provocation becomes a `FLAG-<provocation-id>`.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`:
   - Mirrors `revise_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` to render the canvas (poem renders `opening_image / turn / close`, joke keeps `opening / inciting / obstacle / turn / button`). **Resolves "Prompt builders for poem mode reference poem beat IDs."**
   - The standard-mode revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance (per brief). Add an instruction block referencing the prior `stance` from the previous iteration's finalize.
3. **Update** `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`:
   - Reimplement `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` to call `select_active_checks` with a synthetic creative-joke state. Mark deprecated in a docstring; existing callers keep working.
   - Add `creative_checks_for_robustness(form, robustness)` exported alongside.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py`: route `is_creative_mode(state)` through the creative builders; keep the joke shims.

### Step 10: Stance validator + execute prompt builder (`megaplan/forms/stance.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/stance.py`:
   - `STANCE_HEDGING_VERBS = ("considered","tried","attempted","sought to","aimed to","worked to")`.
   - `STANCE_CLAIM_MARKERS = ("because","refused","chose","killed","kept","fought","insisted","picked")`.
   - `validate_stance(stance: dict) -> list[str]` — returns violations (missing field, >50 words composed, missing first-person marker, hedging hit, no claim marker). Pure function.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`:
   - Generalizes `execute_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` for `sections_written`; reads `form.stance_voice_hint`.
   - Stance instructions in requirements block: shape, ≤50 words composed, first person, names the specific provocation engaged, no hedging verbs, must take a position.
   - Stop-affordance instructions: executor MAY set `stop_signal.requested=true` with a `defense` (1 sentence) explaining why the next pass would damage the piece. Code mode never sets this. **Resolves "Execute prompt for code mode does NOT include stop-affordance."**
3. **Refactor** `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py` to a shim: `_execute_joke_prompt = partial(_execute_creative_prompt, form=get_form("joke"))`, same for batch. Re-export through `__all__`.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128` to dispatch to `_execute_creative_prompt` for `is_creative_mode(state)`.

### Step 11: Stop-affordance at the rework branch (`megaplan/handlers/review.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Audit** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201`. The damaging extra pass happens at line 191 (`return "needs_rework", STATE_FINALIZED, "execute"`).
2. **Add** stop-signal honoring on the rework branch only:
   ```python
   if rework_requested and is_creative_mode(state) and _maker_requested_stop(plan_dir):
       _record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense=_collect_stop_defenses(plan_dir))
       return "success", STATE_DONE, None
   ```
   Place this check between the existing `rework_requested` block and the cap-key lookup so a maker stop short-circuits before the rework cycle counter increments.
3. **Add** helpers:
   - `_maker_requested_stop(plan_dir)`: reads the latest `finalize.json` and returns True if any task has `stance.stop_signal.requested == true`.
   - `_collect_stop_defenses(plan_dir)`: returns the joined `stop_signal.defense` strings.
   - `_record_maker_stop(state, plan_dir, defense)`: writes a `meta.notes` entry and updates `directors_notes.json`.
4. **Resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2.**

### Step 12: Director's notes assembled at merge boundary (`megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:
   - `update_directors_notes_from_merge(plan_dir, state, merged_payload, *, iteration, voice, fired_provocations) -> None`: appends a `passes[]` entry derived from the merged payload's `task_updates[].stance` and `stop_signal` fields. Reads/writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json` atomically.
2. **Hook** into `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`) and `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`: when `is_creative_mode(state)`, call `update_directors_notes_from_merge(...)` with the merged payload. The fired-provocation IDs come from the latest `critique_v<N>.json` `flags` array filtered by `FLAG-` prefix.
3. **Hook** into `handle_critique()` so the *initial* triad is recorded in `directors_notes.json` immediately after critique writes, even when execution hasn't run yet (so iteration N+1's critique can read prior provocation IDs).
4. **Resolves scope-2** (sidecar at merge boundary, not crawler).

### Step 13: Stance validation enforcement (`megaplan/execute/core.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** the merge step (`megaplan/execute/core.py:_merge_batch_results`) to call `validate_stance(task_update.stance)` for each task update with a stance. Violations are stored on the task as `stance_violations: list[str]` (added to `directors_notes.json`'s `stances[]` entries).
2. **Bubble** violations into the next critique pass: the critique prompt builder (Step 9) reads `directors_notes.json` and, if the prior pass had `stance_violations`, prepends a stance-authenticity sub-provocation per the brief: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."
3. **Soft enforcement only** (no hard reject — preserves the brief's "don't grind into nervous mush" principle).
4. **Resolves "Stance authenticity check fires as sub-provocation."**

## Phase 4: Tests + validation

### Step 14: Pure-function unit tests (`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`, `tests/test_forms_registry.py`, `tests/test_provocations.py`, `tests/test_stance_validator.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`**: `is_creative_mode` true for `mode=creative` and `mode=joke`; `creative_form_id` returns `"joke"` for legacy joke state with no form key, returns `state.config.form` for new state, returns `None` for code mode. `is_prose_mode` true for doc/joke/creative.
2. **`tests/test_forms_registry.py`**: `get_form("joke")` and `get_form("poem")` exist; both have ≥3 cuts/forces/sparks; joke has 5 beat IDs, poem has 3; both declare `execution_schema_key == "execution_doc.json"`.
3. **`tests/test_provocations.py`**: light=2, standard=3, robust=3 with rotating voice; `prior_provocation_ids` filters duplicates on standard iteration 2; `over_explained` tag biases selection.
4. **`tests/test_stance_validator.py`**: valid passes; >50 words → violation; hedging verb → violation; third-person → violation; no claim marker → violation.

### Step 15: Workflow + handler integration tests (`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`, `tests/test_workflow_creative.py`, `tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`**: `--mode creative --form joke --output …` succeeds and writes `state.config.form="joke"`; `--mode creative` without `--form` raises `invalid_args`; `--mode creative --form poem --primary-criterion "tightest image"` accepted.
2. **Update** `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`: `--mode joke` still works AND now writes `state.config.form="joke"`.
3. **`tests/test_workflow_creative.py`**: build a synthetic creative-mode state at `STATE_INITIALIZED` with `robustness=standard`; assert `workflow_next()` returns `prep` (not `plan`). Same for joke alias. Code-mode standard still returns `plan`.
4. **`tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`**: build a state with a needs_rework review and a `stop_signal.requested=true` task in finalize; assert `_resolve_review_outcome()` returns `("success", STATE_DONE, None)` and `meta.notes` records the defense. Without the stop signal, the same input returns `("needs_rework", STATE_FINALIZED, "execute")` (existing behavior preserved). Code mode with stop_signal does NOT short-circuit (helper returns False).

### Step 16: Schema + runtime-layout tests (`tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`: call `ensure_runtime_layout()`; assert `.megaplan/schemas/execution_doc.json` exists and includes `stance` + `stop_signal` properties; assert `.megaplan/schemas/directors_notes.json` exists; assert `get_execution_schema_key("creative", "poem") == "execution_doc.json"`.

### Step 17: Prompt builder tests (`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`**:
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=joke, robustness="standard")` mentions exactly 1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark by name.
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=poem, ...)` references poem beats, not screenplay beats.
   - `_execute_creative_prompt` includes stance + stop-affordance instructions.
   - Code-mode `_execute_prompt` does NOT include stop-affordance instructions.
   - Iteration 2 critique excludes provocation IDs from a fixture `directors_notes.json`.
   - Dispatcher: `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_joke, …)` ≡ `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_creative_form_joke, …)`.
   - `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` returns the same output as `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` for joke (back-compat).

### Step 18: End-to-end smoke (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** smoke test with mocked agents:
   - `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md` → prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review.
   - Mocked execute returns a valid stance + `stop_signal.requested=false`. Assert `directors_notes.json` is written via the merge boundary, contains form, primary_criterion, fired provocations, stances.
   - Second run: mocked execute returns `stop_signal.requested=true`, mocked review returns `needs_rework`. Assert review halts with STATE_DONE and `meta.notes` records the defense.

### Step 19: Run focused tests, then full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** unit tests (Steps 14, 16) — fastest, no fixtures.
2. **Run** init/workflow/review tests (Step 15).
3. **Run** prompt builder tests (Step 17).
4. **Run** smoke (Step 18) and existing `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`.
5. **Run** full `pytest tests/` to catch regressions, especially anywhere a `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` site was rewritten.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** (helpers + registry + schemas + workflow) lands first. Every later phase depends on the helpers existing.
2. Inside Phase 1: Step 1 (helpers) → Step 2 (forms registry) → Step 3 (init/CLI) → Step 4 (workflow override) → Step 5 (schemas + runtime layout). Steps 1 and 2 can land in either order; Step 3 depends on both.
3. **Phase 2** (mode-aware sweep) is mechanical and runs after Phase 1. Each replaced site gets its file-local pytest before moving on.
4. **Phase 3** (provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance + sidecar) depends on Steps 1, 2, 6 being done. Step 8 (selection) → Step 9 (critique/revise prompts) → Step 10 (stance + execute prompt) → Step 11 (stop-affordance handler) → Step 12 (sidecar at merge) → Step 13 (stance validation enforcement).
5. **Phase 4 tests** can begin in parallel with Phase 3 once Steps 1-8 are committed (all pure functions are testable on their own).
6. The riskiest moment is Step 6 (mode sweep); land it green before starting Phase 3.

## Validation Order

1. Pure-function unit tests (helpers, registry, selection, stance validator) — no fixtures.
2. Schema + runtime-layout tests — light I/O only.
3. Workflow + handler tests — fixture-based but no agent calls.
4. Prompt builder tests — fixture-based, deterministic.
5. End-to-end smoke tests with mocked agents.
6. Full pytest suite to catch regressions in adjacent code (especially `tests/bakeoff/*` and any file touched by the Phase 2 sweep).

    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "Legacy joke state (mode='joke', no form key) is handled by `creative_form_id()`'s fallback to 'joke', not by a one-time migration. The brief explicitly lifts the back-compat requirement for pre-v0.21 plan files, so this is convenience, not contract.",
    "Forms declare their own `execution_schema_key` (defaulting to `execution_doc.json`). Both joke and poem use `execution_doc.json` since `sections_written` works for any prose-with-beats form.",
    "The `--mode joke` value remains stored verbatim in state (not normalized to 'creative') so that any legacy code or external tooling string-equaling on it still works during the transition. The helpers treat both as creative.",
    "`select_provocations` reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json`; on iteration 1 (no prior file), the prior set is empty and selection is unconstrained.",
    "The mode-aware `_CREATIVE_OVERRIDES` for the workflow lives in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py` next to `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES`. Layering it via `workflow_next()` is less invasive than making `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES` mode-aware and lets code-mode behavior stay untouched.",
    "Stance validator stays soft-enforced: violations log + bubble into the next critique as a stance-authenticity sub-provocation rather than hard-reject. Hard reject would force re-runs for hedging-verb violations and contradict the brief's 'don't grind into nervous mush' principle.",
    "directors_notes.json is JSON only; markdown rendering is a follow-up if requested.",
    "Robust mode rotates a fixed roster (formalist/iconoclast/audience). Model-chosen voices can be a config opt-in later if needed.",
    "`--primary-criterion` is lifted to all `--mode creative` (any form), per the brief's framing of primary criterion as load-bearing for creative work.",
    "Poem (not ad copy) is the second validation form because it stresses the abstraction harder via different beat IDs and a different stance voice.",
    "Step 13's stance validation runs at merge time (so `directors_notes.json`'s `stance_violations` field is populated by the merge boundary, consistent with scope-2). The next critique reads it from the sidecar."
  ],
  "delta_from_previous_percent": 80.44,
  "structure_warnings": []
}

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.\n\n## The provocation engine\n\nThe brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor \u2014 adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:\n\n- **Cuts** \u2014 strip / expose / kill. Example: \"Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling.\"\n- **Forces** \u2014 perform a transformation on the piece. Example: \"Halve it. Write the line that survives.\"\n- **Sparks** \u2014 the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:\n  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)\n  - Substitutions (\"what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene\")\n  - Inversions (\"the version of this that's a confession, not a performance\")\n  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)\n  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)\n  - Hostile claims (\"this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about\" \u2014 refute or absorb)\n  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)\n  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)\n  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)\n\nDefault firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state \u2014 over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + \"who could have made this.\"\n\n## The stance\n\nEvery executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:\n\n- \u226450 words\n- First person\n- Names the specific provocation engaged\n- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)\n- Must take a position someone could disagree with\n\nA sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: \"Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger.\"\n\nStance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a \"director's notes\" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece \u2014 the artifact must stand alone.\n\n## Robustness mapping\n\nCreative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* \u2014 not just more cycles.\n\n- **Light** \u2014 single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance \u22642 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.\n- **Standard** \u2014 one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.\n- **Robust** \u2014 multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.\n\n## Executor stop-affordance\n\nLet the maker signal \"the next pass would damage the piece\" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.\n\n## Generalize joke \u2192 creative-work\n\n- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form\n- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` \u2014 no breakage of existing plans\n- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)\n- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form\n\n## Design branches the prep phase should resolve\n\nThe following are real branches with arguments either way \u2014 settle them in prep, not in code:\n\n1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)\n2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts\n3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type\n4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle\n5. Robustness \u2192 provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds\n6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- New forms beyond the second validation form\n- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)\n- Team-of-makers workflows\n- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)\n\n## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)\n\nSharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something \u2014 and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is \"more rigorous\" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece \u2014 it is what converts the system from \"external pressure\" into \"training the maker as their own provocateur.\"",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

- **Cluster A (correctness-1, FLAG-004):** confirmed `_merge_batch_results()` at `execute/core.py:308-310` only copies `("status", "executor_notes", "sections_written")` for prose modes — `stance` and `stop_signal` need to be added to `finalize.json` schema AND to `merge_fields`.
- **Cluster B (correctness-2, callers):** confirmed `_resolve_review_outcome()` call sites at `review.py:332-337` and `483-488` pass no `plan_dir` — the resolver signature needs the parameter.
- **Cluster C (FLAG-005, issue_hints):** confirmed `WORKFLOW[STATE_PLANNED]` always offers `critique`; light only overrides INITIALIZED/CRITIQUED/EXECUTED. Also caught a v2 bug: the workflow override I described used the wrong target state — `prep` transitions to `STATE_PREPPED`, not `STATE_INITIALIZED`. Fixing both.
- **Cluster D (scope):** committing to a single hook (aggregate only) to avoid double-append.
